# Adversarial Systems Audit Log

Purpose: spend small daily passes finding cross-module failures that ordinary code review misses.

Focus areas:
- Duplicated sources of truth: config, DB state, env, dashboard controls, scripts.
- Shared mutable state across scheduler threads, scanners, receiver, risk, executor, and position monitor.
- Silent failures: `except Exception: pass`, debug-only errors, stale state, missing fields, network/API timeouts.
- Gates that should run but do not: market hours, reviewer mode, strategy enabled flags, broker availability, risk, settlement, cooldowns.
- Missing/dirty payload fields between scanners, senders, receivers, strategies, and execution.
- Recovery behavior after reboot, bad API key, slow network, stale DB value, locked DB, unavailable broker, and partial order failure.

## Case Studies Already Found

### 2026-06-24 - Stock Scan Overlap
- Symptom: stock scans showed start events every 60s while old scans were still active for 4000+ seconds.
- Root cause: `bot_engine._run_singleton_job()` had a stock-scan exception that allowed overlapping stock scans.
- Fix: restored singleton skip behavior for all jobs.
- Files: `bot_engine.py`

### 2026-06-24 - Alpaca Cash Split-Brain
- Symptom: Alpaca dashboard showed about $99k cash, but stock trades were rejected with `No available cash on alpaca: $0.00`.
- Root cause: `core.broker_manager._sync_alpaca()` used stale `registered_brokers` credentials from DB instead of current encrypted/config credentials. Sync failed silently, leaving stale `broker_balance_alpaca` with impossible invested value.
- Fix: Alpaca sync now uses current `config.py` credentials and logs sync failures as warnings. Sync repaired state to balance/available about $99,154.91 and invested $0.
- Files: `core/broker_manager.py`

## Current Queue

1. Audit all broker/key/config source-of-truth paths.
2. Audit risk-manager broker cash checks against stale or impossible broker states.
3. Audit scheduler singleton handling and long-running jobs.
4. Audit stock scan pipeline from market-hours gate to strategy signals to execution.
5. Audit Claude/Haiku reviewer modes and any strict/advisory mismatch.
6. Audit scanner enabled flags and DB/script naming mismatches.
7. Audit payload fields from breakout/funding/gap scanners into receiver and trade records.

Its last update was 2026-06-25 12:48:28, versus Alpaca's successful startup sync at 2026-06-25 05:53:37. The prior Alpaca stale-cash symptom is also present in the 2026-06-22 log digest.
- Finding fixed: `RiskManager.approve_trade()` caught every per-broker cash-check exception and continued with theoretical capital. A locked/corrupt DB or state-read failure could therefore approve an order without a trustworthy broker cash limit.
- Files touched: `core/risk_manager.py`.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile core\\risk_manager.py` passed. A focused runtime check replaced `get_broker_balance()` with a raising function; `approve_trade(..., broker_name="alpaca")` returned `approved=False` with `Broker balance unavailable for alpaca`.

Then verify no execution path can use stale broker cash after a sync failure.

### 2026-06-29 - Live Kraken Balance Failures Now Fail Closed
- Scope inspected: continued from the live-mode Kraken safety handoff. Checked `core/broker_manager.py` `_sync_kraken()`, `BrokerManager.get_broker_balance()`, `update_broker_balance()`, `sync_all_balances()`, `core/risk_manager.py` per-broker approval, `core/trade_executor.py` broker hint and post-open balance updates, `bot_engine.py` capital sync, current `bot_state` broker rows in `data/trading_bot.db`, and recent `logs/bot.log` Kraken/broker-balance/capital-sync lines.

A zero/empty Kraken USD response would write a zero balance, but without marking why that broker cash source was untrusted.

A focused runtime check monkeypatched `ccxt.kraken` and `config.KRAKEN_PAPER_MODE=False` against a fake in-memory DB: synthetic live outage marked Kraken unavailable and preserved `last_known_available`; `approve_trade(..., broker_name="kraken")` returned `approved=False` with `Broker balance unavailable for kraken`; synthetic zero USD/ZUSD balance marked unavailable; synthetic good ZUSD balance restored `status="ok"` and `available=100.0`. The real SQLite state was inspected but not mutated by the synthetic test.
- Next starting point: audit the high-frequency Kraken stop-order ratchet path. Recent logs show repeated cancel/place cycles for the same paper stop roughly every 30 seconds; inspect `core/position_monitor.py` and `core/kraken_executor.py` for duplicate stop replacement, idempotency, and live-mode rate-limit/order-state safety before any `KRAKEN_PAPER_MODE=False` switch.

### 2026-06-30 - Kraken Stop Ratchet Ignores Rounded No-Ops
- Scope inspected: continued from the high-frequency Kraken stop-order ratchet handoff. Checked `core/position_monitor.py` universal profit-ratchet and two-bar trailing stop exchange update paths, `core/kraken_executor.py` stop cancel/place behavior, `core/trade_executor.py` initial Kraken stop placement, recent `logs/bot.log` stop-ratchet lines, and current open trade / broker stop state in `data/trading_bot.db`.
- Evidence: recent bot log lines for open paper Kraken trade `BMB/USD` showed `[PROFIT RATCHET]` followed by `[PAPER] Order cancelled`, `[PAPER] Stop order placed`, and `[RATCHET PAPER]` roughly every 30 seconds for the same rounded stop value (`0.001979`) and same fake stop id (`PAPER-STOP-BMB/USD-19`). The live path uses the same `KrakenExecutor.update_stop_order()` sequence, which cancels the old order before placing the replacement. Current DB state still has `BMB/USD` open with `stop_loss=0.001979`, `broker=kraken`, and `stop_order_id='PAPER-STOP-BMB/USD-19'`.
- Finding fixed: the profit-ratchet path compared unrounded candidate stops against the rounded DB stop, then persisted and sent the rounded value to Kraken. For low-priced crypto, a candidate like `0.0019793` was greater than stored `0.001979` but rounded back to `0.001979`, causing repeated no-op DB writes and stop cancel/place calls. In live mode this would unnecessarily hammer Kraken and briefly remove the exchange-side protective stop on every no-op replacement attempt.
- Files touched: `core/position_monitor.py`, `docs/adversarial_systems_audit_log.md`.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile core\\position_monitor.py` passed. A focused precision check confirmed `round(0.0019793, 6) == 0.001979`, so the patched long comparison does not update, while `round(0.0019801, 6) == 0.001980`, so a real ratchet still updates.
- Next starting point: audit `KrakenExecutor.update_stop_order()` live-mode atomicity and idempotency. Specifically verify whether cancel failure should block replacement, whether place failure after successful cancel should mark the DB stop order id as stale/unprotected, and whether live stop replacement needs a rate-limit/backoff key in `bot_state`.

### 2026-07-01 - Kraken Stop Replacement Blocks Duplicate Live Stops
- Scope inspected: continued from the `KrakenExecutor.update_stop_order()` live-mode atomicity handoff. Checked `core/kraken_executor.py` stop cancel/place behavior, the ratchet callers in `core/position_monitor.py`, initial stop placement in `core/trade_executor.py`, recent stop logs in `logs/bot.log`, and current open Kraken trade / stop state in `data/trading_bot.db`.
- Evidence: `update_stop_order()` previously called `cancel_order()` and ignored its boolean result before placing the replacement. In live mode, a failed cancel for an existing stop could therefore leave the old protective stop live while creating a second stop at the new price. The current DB has two open Kraken paper trades (`BMB/USD` and `BAL/USD`) with stop ids, and no `bot_state` stop/backoff keys. Recent log evidence still shows the pre-fix paper no-op loop for `BMB/USD`; no live cancel failure was observed locally, so the fix is based on the shared live/paper call path and the explicit ignored return value.
- Finding fixed: live stop replacement now blocks placement when a known old stop cannot be cancelled. If there is no old stop id, placement is still allowed so a missing protective stop can be restored. If a live cancel succeeds but replacement placement fails, the executor logs `[STOP REPLACE UNPROTECTED]` so the monitor path exposes the temporary unprotected state instead of failing silently.
- Files touched: `core/kraken_executor.py`, `docs/adversarial_systems_audit_log.md`.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile core\\kraken_executor.py` passed. A focused in-memory probe of `KrakenExecutor.update_stop_order()` confirmed a cancel failure returns `None` and does not call `place_stop_order()`, a successful cancel proceeds to placement and returns the new id, and an empty old stop id still places a stop.
- Next starting point: wire stop replacement failure state into persistent DB/runtime state. Specifically inspect whether `core/position_monitor.py` should clear or mark `stop_order_id` when `update_stop_order()` logs `[STOP REPLACE UNPROTECTED]`, and whether a `bot_state` backoff key is needed to avoid repeated live replacement attempts after Kraken placement errors.

### 2026-07-02 - Kraken Stop Replacement Clears Stale Cancelled IDs
- Scope inspected: continued from the stop replacement persistence handoff. Checked `core/kraken_executor.py` return semantics, both Kraken ratchet caller paths in `core/position_monitor.py`, the `data/database.py` order-id/state helpers, recent stop-replacement logs, and current open Kraken trade state in `data/trading_bot.db`.
- Evidence: after the 2026-07-01 fix, `KrakenExecutor.update_stop_order()` only logged `[STOP REPLACE UNPROTECTED]` when a live cancel succeeded but replacement placement failed, then returned `None`. Both monitor callers treated `None` the same as "old stop still intact" and left the cancelled `stop_order_id` in the trade row. The next monitor pass would then try to cancel that stale id; if Kraken rejected it as already gone, the executor would skip placement and the bot could get stuck with no exchange stop until another path manually restored it. Current local DB remains paper mode with open Kraken trades `BAL/USD` and `BMB/USD`, both carrying paper stop ids; no live unprotected event was observed locally.
- Finding fixed: cancelled-but-not-replaced live stops now have a distinct return state. `update_stop_order()` returns an empty string after logging `[STOP REPLACE UNPROTECTED]`. Both monitor caller paths now clear the stale `stop_order_id`, set `kraken_stop_unprotected_{trade_id}` in `bot_state` with the cancelled id and target stop, and reset that state to `0` when a later exchange stop update succeeds. Software stop enforcement remains the fallback while the exchange stop is absent.
- Files touched: `core/kraken_executor.py`, `core/position_monitor.py`, `docs/adversarial_systems_audit_log.md`.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile core\\kraken_executor.py core\\position_monitor.py` passed. A focused in-memory probe confirmed a successful live cancel plus failed replacement returns `""`, and `_mark_kraken_stop_unprotected()` clears the stale trade stop id while writing the expected `bot_state` alert payload without touching the real SQLite DB.
- Next starting point: audit whether `kraken_stop_unprotected_{trade_id}` needs a timed retry/backoff path. Start in `core/position_monitor.py` around the two `update_stop_order()` callers, then inspect `bot_state` for any `kraken_stop_unprotected_` keys after a live-mode failure or synthetic integration test. Verify the monitor can restore a missing exchange stop without hammering Kraken if placement keeps failing.

### 2026-07-03 - Missing Kraken Stops Restore With Backoff
- Scope inspected: continued from the `kraken_stop_unprotected_{trade_id}` retry/backoff handoff. Checked `core/position_monitor.py` around both `update_stop_order()` callers, the cleared-stop marker path, the main monitor ordering around live Kraken reconciliation and stop-loss enforcement, `core/kraken_executor.py` stop placement/replacement return semantics, `data/database.py` `bot_state` and `stop_order_id` helpers, recent `logs/bot.log` stop-order evidence, and current `data/trading_bot.db` Kraken stop state.
- Evidence: current SQLite state has no `kraken_stop_unprotected_%` rows and one open paper Kraken trade (`BMB/USD`) with `stop_order_id='PAPER-STOP-BMB/USD-19'`, so no live incident was present to repair. The code still had a gap from the prior fix: when a live cancel succeeded but replacement placement failed, `_mark_kraken_stop_unprotected()` cleared `stop_order_id`, but the monitor only called `update_stop_order()` during later stop ratchets/trailing-stop changes. If price stopped moving enough to create a new stop update, the trade could remain software-stop-only indefinitely; if placement kept failing, a naive restore loop would retry every monitor pass.
- Finding fixed: open crypto trades with a positive software `stop_loss` and missing `stop_order_id` now attempt to restore a Kraken exchange stop after live reconciliation and before stop-loss checks. Restore attempts use the existing `kraken_stop_unprotected_{trade_id}` state with exponential backoff (`60s` base, capped at `15m`) so repeated placement failures do not hammer Kraken. Successful restore writes the new `stop_order_id` and clears the marker to `0`; failed restore records `attempts`, `last_attempt_ts`, `next_retry_sec`, symbol, and target stop while software stop enforcement continues every pass.
- Files touched: `core/position_monitor.py`, `docs/adversarial_systems_audit_log.md`.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile core\\position_monitor.py` passed. A focused fake-DB/fake-executor probe confirmed `_maybe_restore_missing_kraken_stop()` writes a new stop id and clears `kraken_stop_unprotected_T1` on success; on placement failure it records a marker and suppresses an immediate second call via backoff without touching the real SQLite DB.
- Next starting point: audit whether partial-close Kraken stop refresh needs the same failure-state/backoff treatment. Start at `core/position_monitor.py` `_maybe_take_scanner_partial()`: it directly calls `place_stop_order()` after a partial close and only logs on failure. Verify whether a failed post-partial stop refresh leaves a cleared or stale exchange stop id, and decide whether it should call the same missing-stop restore/marker path.

### 2026-07-14 - Kraken Recovery Utilities Still Bypass Reconciliation Holds (Approval Required)
- Scope inspected: resumed the persisted 2026-07-13 recovery handoff rather than the stale file queue. Checked the two documented STUCK-clear utilities (`Scripts/clear_stuck_trades.py`, `Scripts/ClearStuckTrades.py`), `Scripts/force_close_paper_trades.py`, the live Kraken unresolved-order gate in `core/trade_executor.py`, marker persistence, recovery documentation, SQLite schema/state, `logs/bot.log`, and `reports/night_watch/latest_digest.json`.
- Evidence: `core/trade_executor.py` blocks a new crypto entry whenever `kraken_unresolved_symbol_{symbol}` exists, explicitly stating that manual reconciliation is required. In contrast, `clear_stuck_trades.py` sets `close_stuck_{trade_id}=0` and `close_attempts_{trade_id}=0` for every open trade; `ClearStuckTrades.py` deletes those recovery keys; and `force_close_paper_trades.py` closes every open crypto DB row at entry/PnL zero and deletes its close flags. None checks a symbol unresolved marker or the approval-handoff partial/full reconciliation marker families before changing recovery state. `docs/TECHNICAL_REFERENCE.md` and `docs/StuckTradesInstructions.txt` direct operators to use these utilities without that warning.
- Runtime evidence: verified the actual `bot_state` schema (`key`, `value`, `updated`) before querying. Current DB has zero open trades and zero `kraken_partial_reconcile_%`, `kraken_full_reconcile_%`, and `kraken_unresolved_symbol_%` rows, so no incident is active. Eight `close_stuck_%=1` rows remain, but each maps to a closed or deleted historical trade, not an open position. `bot.log` was last written 2026-07-12 11:41 PT; `latest_digest.json` is stale (generated 2026-06-22); no local unresolved-order JSONL exists.
- Verification: `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe -m py_compile Scripts\\clear_stuck_trades.py Scripts\\ClearStuckTrades.py Scripts\\force_close_paper_trades.py core\\trade_executor.py core\\kraken_executor.py` passed (only existing legacy invalid-escape warning). `venv312\\Scripts\\python.exe Scripts\\test_kraken_fill_quantity.py` passed 30/30, including unresolved symbol-block persistence/idempotency tests. The suite does not execute the three recovery utilities, so it cannot catch this bypass.
- Approval-only minimal patch prepared (not applied): add one small local `has_active_kraken_reconciliation_marker(conn, trade_id, symbol)` check in each utility before clearing `close_stuck`/attempt state or closing the crypto trade. It must treat any non-empty/nonzero marker among `kraken_partial_reconcile_{trade_id}`, `kraken_full_reconcile_{trade_id}`, and `kraken_unresolved_symbol_{symbol}` as a skip, print the blocking key, and preserve all DB state. In `clear_stuck_trades.py`, retain the read connection through the loop solely for the marker query; the existing `db.set_state()` writes remain unchanged. In `force_close_paper_trades.py`, run the guard before its `UPDATE trades` statement so an unresolved trade cannot be force-closed. Correct the two recovery docs to say that marker-bearing Kraken trades require live fill reconciliation first; the scripts must never clear the marker themselves.
- Seeded validation plan for that approval patch: create isolated temporary SQLite copies with one ordinary trade and one each with partial, full, and symbol markers; run all three utilities against each copy; assert ordinary clear/close behavior remains, guarded trades retain the marker, close flags, and `status='open'`; then compile the scripts and rerun the 30 Kraken tests. Do not exercise the utilities against the production DB.
- Conclusion: high-severity latent manual-recovery bypass remains. The active execution gate is correctly fail-closed, but documented operator scripts can erase its related close freeze or close a potentially live/partially filled crypto record without broker-fills reconciliation. No production code, recovery docs, or DB rows were changed in this pass; the only worktree change made by this audit is this handoff entry. Existing unrelated `docs/CommandNotes.txt` modification was preserved.
- Exact next starting point: obtain Linda approval for the narrow three-script reconciliation-marker guards and documentation correction; then apply the isolated seeded partial/full/symbol/ordinary tests, and only after that implement/audit idempotent live Kraken zero/full/partial broker-fills reconciliation before any marker, halt, or DB-close clear.

### 2026-07-14 - Kraken Recovery Reconciliation Guards Implemented
- Approval received: Linda authorized the narrow recovery-script and documentation patch described above.
- Finding fixed: `Scripts/clear_stuck_trades.py`, `Scripts/ClearStuckTrades.py`, and `Scripts/force_close_paper_trades.py` now check `kraken_partial_reconcile_{trade_id}`, `kraken_full_reconcile_{trade_id}`, and `kraken_unresolved_symbol_{symbol}` before changing close flags or trade status. Any active nonzero/nonempty marker causes a fail-closed skip that prints the exact blocking key and preserves the trade, marker, and recovery state.
- Import safety: each recovery utility now exposes `main()` behind `if __name__ == "__main__"`, allowing isolated validation without executing recovery work during import. Normal command-line behavior is retained.
- Documentation corrected: `docs/TECHNICAL_REFERENCE.md` and `docs/StuckTradesInstructions.txt` now require live Kraken fill reconciliation first, explain the `SKIPPED reconciliation hold` message, and explicitly prohibit clearing reconciliation markers through recovery utilities.
- Deterministic validation: added `Scripts/test_recovery_reconciliation_guards.py`. It builds disposable SQLite databases containing an ordinary trade plus partial-, full-, and symbol-marker trades, then exercises all three utilities. The ordinary trade retains each utility's previous clear/close behavior; all guarded trades retain `status='open'`, marker rows, and close flags. Result: 3/3 passed.
- Regression verification: all four changed/new scripts and the two Kraken execution modules passed `py_compile`; `Scripts/test_kraken_fill_quantity.py` passed 30/30. Tests used temporary databases and did not execute a recovery utility against `data/trading_bot.db`.
- Next starting point: complete the older partial-close stop-refresh audit at `_maybe_take_scanner_partial()`, then design idempotent live Kraken zero/full/partial broker-fill reconciliation before any marker, halt, or DB-close clear.

### 2026-07-26 - Approved Scanner-Partial Kraken Reconciliation Patch Implemented
- Approval basis: Linda approved the repeated scanner-partial finding in the current local audit handoff, including the 2026-07-26 08:34 PT entry. The repeated July 19-26 entries describe the same underlying defect; the previously approved recovery-utility finding was already implemented and remained covered by its regression suite.
- Finding fixed: the scanner-partial-only Kraken path now canonicalizes CCXT symbols and raw Kraken pairs, including `XETHZUSD` and `XXBTZUSD`, and returns a structured cancellation result. An open-order fetch failure, unparseable open pair, or target-order cancellation failure is treated as ambiguous: no market close or accounting is attempted, `kraken_partial_reconcile_{trade_id}` is persisted, and trading is halted for manual live reconciliation.
- Fill reconciliation: the partial-close market response is retained and passed through the bounded terminal fill resolver. Only an exact confirmed nonzero broker fill is recorded; terminal zero is retryable after `KRAKEN_PARTIAL_ZERO_RETRY_SEC`; unresolved, invalid, or submission-unknown outcomes create a reconciliation hold; and a broker-confirmed full fill is routed through the existing DB-only full-close reconciliation helper instead of partial accounting.
- Residual protection: `scanner_partial_taken_{trade_id}` is written only after the replacement stop ID is persisted. A falsey replacement clears the stale stop ID, records `kraken_stop_unprotected_{trade_id}`, and enters the existing exponential restore/backoff path. A later successful restore marks the already-recorded scanner partial complete, making the monitor rerun idempotent.
- Scope preserved: normal full Kraken exits and their legacy cancellation helper were not altered. Tests used fake exchanges and disposable SQLite databases; `data/trading_bot.db` was not opened or changed.
- Files touched: `config.py`, `core/kraken_executor.py`, `core/trade_executor.py`, `core/position_monitor.py`, `Scripts/test_kraken_scanner_partial.py`, and this audit log.
- Verification: production modules and the new acceptance script passed `py_compile`; fatal-only Flake8 checks (`E9,F63,F7,F82`) passed. `Scripts/test_kraken_scanner_partial.py` passed 7/7 coordinator-level cases for raw-pair identity, cancellation ambiguity/no market/no accounting plus durable hold, confirmed exact partial plus idempotency, terminal zero plus stop refresh/retry, terminal full DB reconciliation, unresolved fill hold, and falsey stop placement followed by recovery. Existing `Scripts/test_kraken_fill_quantity.py` passed 30/30 and `Scripts/test_recovery_reconciliation_guards.py` passed 3/3.
- Next starting point: audit normal full Kraken exits separately before any future live-mode switch; that path was intentionally outside this approved scanner-partial patch.

### 2026-07-31 - Approved Normal Full Kraken Exit Reconciliation Patch Implemented
- Approval basis: Linda explicitly approved the narrow normal-full-exit patch after the July 30-31 audit handoffs. The already-verified scanner-partial path was left unchanged.
- Finding fixed: normal `KrakenExecutor.close_position()` now reuses exact CCXT/raw Kraken pair cancellation, including `XETHZUSD` and `XXBTZUSD`, and returns a structured terminal-fill result. Fetch/cancel ambiguity blocks market submission; submitted orders retain their response and pass through `_resolve_order_fill()`.
- Full-close reconciliation: `TradeExecutor.close_trade()` closes the DB trade and applies tax/risk/capital changes only after an exact confirmed full quantity and confirmed average fill price. Zero, partial, overfill, invalid-price, and unresolved outcomes persist `kraken_full_reconcile_{trade_id}`, preserve DB/capital state, and prevent duplicate close submission. Confirmed residuals receive a replacement stop; if that fails, the existing unprotected-stop state is marked for manual reconciliation. The stop monitor does not guess a replacement quantity while the full-reconciliation hold is active.

Each surviving patrol path is copied again in `createRuntimeSentry()`, mapped on every active AI tick by `sentryPatrolWaypoints()`, and fully iterated in `renderSentryPatrolPathsMap()`. A pasted/imported map containing one or more syntactically valid sentries with a very large finite coordinate path can therefore monopolize the browser UI thread during import and persistently during flight/map rendering. This remains exploitable after a segment-count admission cap because path cardinality is independent of `map.segments`. This is a local map-import robustness/availability issue, not remote code execution, a production-trading defect, or evidence of an active incident.
- Supporting validator gaps: the same sentry sanitizer only checks truthiness of `spawn.point/right/down` before `copyVec()`/`normalize()`; it does not require finite vectors. `sanitizeRocketCacheList()` likewise accepts any truthy `raw.point` and normalizes optional vectors without finite checks. JSON string coordinates or overlarge numeric values can therefore propagate `NaN`/non-finite geometry into runtime sentry/cache reconstruction. Imported targets have the same superficial gap, but `rebuildTunnelTargets()` immediately regenerates targets from validated segment geometry, so this pass does not treat targets as a persistent runtime defect. Portal frames have finite-vector checks already, though imported portal IDs are not deduplicated; no material impact beyond ambiguous UI lookup was established in this time-box.
- Approval-only minimal patch diff (not applied): next to `SENTRY_MAX_COUNT`, add a named, conservative `SENTRY_MAX_PATROL_POINTS` (for example 256) after checking any user map samples. Add one small `sanitizeSentryPath()` helper which only considers the first capped number of supplied stages, keeps finite points, and turns non-finite `lingerSec` into zero; call it from `sanitizeSentryList()` and the existing sentry-edit save path so imported and UI-authored data share the rule. In `sanitizeSentryList()`, require finite spawn point/right/down (and finite supplied forward if present), and deduplicate generated/imported sentry IDs with the existing make-ID loop pattern. In `sanitizeRocketCacheList()`, reject non-finite point/orientation inputs before copying them. Focused regression: a normal finite patrol path is retained; a 257-point path is truncated/rejected according to the chosen UX; invalid sentry/cache coordinates are dropped; duplicate sentry IDs become distinct; existing 665-segment bundle loads unchanged. Keep the already-proposed `MAP_IMPORT_MAX_SEGMENTS` guard as a separate but compatible central import admission check. Do not apply either guard without Linda's approval.
- Conclusion and unresolved risks: the prior unbounded segment import concern remains open, and this pass added a distinct, plausible sentry-path/vector validation availability defect. The game currently has no transactional rollback if a later import/rebuild/storage operation throws after `applyTunnelMap()` has begun mutating live builder state; no deterministic throw path was reproduced without intentionally loading a hostile map, so that remains a lower-confidence follow-up rather than a validated finding. No browser runtime reproduction was performed because it could freeze the active client and the narrow fixes are approval-only.
- Exact next starting point: obtain approval before editing the dirty game file. If approved, implement only the central segment-cap plus sentry-path/finite-vector admission guards, then browser-verify normal bundled-map loading, malformed entity rejection, bounded path behavior, and recovery without partial live-map corruption. Thus two nearby capped endpoints can create a radius-1.12× bubble that makes their normally solid cap region collision-free. Before accepting a distinct-segment member, require both endpoint members to be uncapped, require round/rect face members to be declared open, and require the existing `tunnelSegmentsConnected()` topology predicate. If classification is ambiguous, omit the bubble (preserve collision) rather than grant free flight. @@

```diff
+        junctionBubbleMembersCanConnect(pts[i], pts[j])) {
         members.push(pts[j]);
```

### 2026-08-31 10:44 PT - EAT/EMT Open-Position Live Valuation and Runner Status

- User-visible defect and cause: both Web Dashboard Open Positions and Manage Positions consume `/api/open_positions`. Its primary `PositionMonitor.get_positions_summary()` path asked the stock/crypto scanner to price `asset_class='future'`; a missing quote fell back to entry and displayed exactly $0 unrealized P&L. The same path calculated `(current-entry) * quantity`, which omits the futures point value. The DB fallback repeated both errors. EAT/EMT strategy state already exposes an authoritative scanner-side active trade, live price, contract metadata, remaining quantity, partial realized P&L, runner stop, and bridge heartbeat.
- Narrow implementation: `web_dashboard.py` now identifies only EAT/EMT futures rows, requests the small per-symbol scanner strategy-status payload, and accepts a mark only when its active durable trade ID exactly matches the DB row. Futures unrealized P&L uses `price points * point_value * remaining contracts`; short direction is inverted. A dead bridge labels the last mark `STALE`; a missing/mismatched strategy trade labels it `UNAVAILABLE` instead of silently representing the entry fallback as live. EAT partial exits now expose remaining/original contracts, banked P&L, open P&L, combined trade P&L, `RUNNER` state, `TP1 FILLED`, and the active raised runner stop. The current EAT engine has TP1 plus a trailing runner, not a fixed TP2, so the UI states that honestly. EMT retains its active bracket and contract count. Both dashboard panels render the new status. No order submission, manual-close routing, strategy entry/exit behavior, broker mode, halt counter, win/loss accounting, configuration, or database row was changed.

### 2026-09-07 14:11 PT - Astral Normal-Profile Render-Slowdown Triage

- Scope and live setup: Linda authorized starting the real normal-profile Astral environment. Stopped only the two stale Python HTTP-server launch chains that were serving empty responses, then ran `DecentClone\\start_astral.bat` with its project `venv312` server. The existing normal Chrome profile reached active `branch flight` with the HD tunnel assets visible. No game source, saved map/profile, Chrome setting, driver setting, database, broker, or unrelated worktree file was changed by this investigation.
- Measured environment evidence: Chrome's active GPU process is launched with `--use-angle=d3d11-warp`, which selects Microsoft's CPU software D3D11 renderer rather than the installed NVIDIA GTX 745. Over a five-second live sample that GPU process consumed 4.48 CPU-seconds per wall-second; the current renderer process consumed 0.41 CPU-seconds per wall-second. This is direct evidence of a browser-wide software-rendering bottleneck and is sufficient to make the reported 1/10 to 1/20 render-speed collapse plausible. It is not evidence of a texture asset failure or of a regression in the approved hard-bend connector repair.
- Follow-up restart evidence: Linda reopened the localhost game in a new Astral tab at 14:14 PT, but Chrome retained the same September 4 browser/GPU process and its `d3d11-warp` command line; it still consumed 3.87 CPU-seconds per wall-second. The new Astral renderer used 0.39 CPU-seconds per wall-second and a restored Sling tab remained in the profile. This was a tab/window reopen, not a full Chrome process restart, so it cannot clear or retest the WARP condition.
- Clean-session confirmation: after Linda killed every Chrome process and launched only `http://localhost:8127/`, Chrome created a new GPU process at 14:20 PT and the browser exposed exactly one Astral tab. The live game reports `rock walls: WebGL HD JPG`; its `webgl-canvas` is visible at 2084x948, and renderer console warnings/errors are empty. Despite that clean WebGL success, the new GPU process still starts with `--use-angle=d3d11-warp` and consumed 3.92 CPU-seconds per wall-second; the current Astral renderer consumed 0.45. The slowdown is therefore not a vector fallback, stale Chrome process, texture failure, or observed Astral WebGL warning—it is a persistent Chrome/GPU software-rendering condition.
- Configuration narrowing: read-only checks show Chrome's `Local State` has `hardware_acceleration_mode.enabled=true`, and neither HKCU nor HKLM Chrome policy supplies `HardwareAccelerationModeEnabled`, `Disable3DAPIs`, or `EnableHardwareOverlays`. Windows reports `NVIDIA GeForce GTX 745` as `OK` at 1920x1080 with driver `32.0.15.8163`. Chrome nevertheless selects WARP, which now points to its GPU compatibility/blocklist decision or a Chrome/driver interaction. Exact next evidence is the user-visible `chrome://gpu` Graphics Feature Status and Problems Detected report; do not alter Astral safety/render code before that report establishes why Chrome rejected hardware acceleration.
- Root cause (Chrome GPU report, 14:24 PT): the command line contains `--flag-switches-begin --disable-quic --ignore-gpu-blocklist --use-angle=d3d11-warp --flag-switches-end`. `--use-angle=d3d11-warp` explicitly forces ANGLE to the Microsoft Basic Render Driver; the report identifies it as the active GPU (`GPU0=Google Inc. (Microsoft)`, `Display type=ANGLE_D3D11_WARP`, `Software Rendering=Yes`). It separately detects `NVIDIA GeForce GTX 745` and lists its D3D12 backend as available, so the hardware is visible but deliberately not selected. Chrome's "WebGL: Hardware accelerated" label means the WARP-backed WebGL path is active; it does not mean the NVIDIA GPU is being used. No Astral code change is warranted. Exact next action: reset the Chrome flag `chrome://flags/#use-angle` to Default (and reset `chrome://flags/#ignore-gpu-blocklist` to Default rather than overriding Chrome's safety list), fully relaunch Chrome, then recheck `chrome://gpu` for an NVIDIA active GPU before benchmarking Astral. `--disable-quic` is unrelated to rendering and was not changed.
- Resolution confirmation (14:32 PT): after resetting Choose ANGLE graphics backend and disabling Override software rendering list, Chrome's command line no longer contains either WARP or ignore-GPU-blocklist switch. `GL_RENDERER` now identifies `ANGLE (NVIDIA, NVIDIA GeForce GTX 745 ... Direct3D11 ... D3D11-32.0.15.8163)`. The Chrome configuration root cause is resolved. At this handoff Chrome has only the GPU report tab open; exact next starting point is open `http://localhost:8127/` in this corrected session and measure normal-flight responsiveness. If normal speed is restored, close this rendering incident with no game code change; if it remains slow, profile Astral's live WebGL draw workload only under the NVIDIA renderer.
- Final live validation (14:34 PT): the reopened one-tab localhost Astral session is active in `branch flight`, reports `rock walls: WebGL HD JPG`, and shows its WebGL canvas at 2084x941; its renderer console has no warnings/errors. The new GPU process carries no WARP or ignore-blocklist switch and consumed 0.14 CPU-seconds per wall-second over five seconds, down from the WARP process's 3.92; the active Astral renderer used 1.02 CPU-seconds per wall-second while drawing normally. This validates the intended GPU-backed path and resolves the reported software-rendering incident. No Astral performance, texture, collision, map, or safety-code patch is needed. Preserve the approved hard-bend connector repair; only revisit live WebGL workload profiling if Linda reports continuing perceptible slowdown with the NVIDIA renderer.
- Astral code-path evidence and conclusion: `TUNNEL_TEXTURE_MODE` starts as `webgl`; it can become vector only when WebGL initialization fails, the context is lost, 45 consecutive zero-draw frames occur, or three blank-frame samples occur. The foreground input/HUD canvas is always `fallback-canvas`, so its accessibility label alone cannot establish the active wall renderer. The black-frame watchdog does synchronous `gl.readPixels` sampling (up to eight points every 20 rendered frames), which is an intentional visual-safety guard and especially expensive under WARP, but there is not yet evidence that it caused the current slowdown or that it triggered a vector failover. Do not weaken or remove it on this evidence.
- Exact next starting point: outside the game, inspect why Chrome is forced to `d3d11-warp` (hardware-acceleration setting, command-line/enterprise policy, driver availability, or GPU-process state), then restart Chrome and reopen the normal localhost profile before assessing FPS. ### 2026-09-07 15:05 PT - Astral Section-Texture Crossfade Applied; Closed-Tube Escape Queued

Tiled and distance-run paths keep one sampler and their existing exact coordinates.
